6803 CDE6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
6804 CDE8 0450  20 	b *r0
6805               	;
6806               	; 	' ======================================================================
6807               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
6808               	; 	' ======================================================================
6809               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
6810               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
6811               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
6812               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
6813               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
6814               	; 	'
6815               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
6816               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
6817               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
6818               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
6819               	; 	' broken.
6820               	; 	'
6821               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
6822               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
6823               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
6824               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
6825               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
6826               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
6827               	; 	'
6828               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
6829               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
6830               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
6831               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
6832               	; coll_obst:
6833               cvb_COLL_OBST
6834               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the

     CDEC 215A

7349               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
7350               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
7351               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
7352               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
7353               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.
7354               	; 	kcol = C_KHAT

7376               cv548
7377               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
7378               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
7379               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
7380               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
7381               	; 	'
7382               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
7383               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
7384               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
7385               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
7386               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
7387               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
7388               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
7389               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

8084               	;
8085               	; 	' ======================================================================
8086               	; 	' THE SCANNER
8087               	; 	' ======================================================================
8088               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
8089               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
8090               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
8091               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
8092               	; 	'
8093               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
8094               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
8095               	; 	'
8096               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
8097               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
8098               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
8099               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
8100               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
8101               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
8102               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
8103               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
8104               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
8105               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
8106               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
8107               	; esc_tick:
8108               cvb_ESC_TICK
8109               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
8110               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
8111               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
8112               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
8113               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
8114 D8DA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8DC 2149     
8115 D8DE 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     D8E0 0100     
8116 D8E2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8117 D8E4 1402  14 	jhe cv612
8118 D8E6 0460  28 	b @cv611
     D8E8 D8FE     
8119               cv612
8120               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
8121 D8EA D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     D8EC 2149     
8122 D8EE 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     D8F0 0700     
8123 D8F2 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
8124 D8F4 1A02  14 	jl cv614
8125 D8F6 0460  28 	b @cv613
     D8F8 D8FE     
8126               cv614
8127 D8FA C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8128 D8FC 0450  20 	b *r0
8129               cv613
8130               	; 	END IF
8131               cv611
8132               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
8133               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
8134               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
8135               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
8136               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
8137               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
8138               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
8139               	; 	'
8140               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
8141               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
8142               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
8143               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
8144               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
8145               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
8146               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
8147               	; 	escp = escp + 1

8363 DAC8 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8364 DACA 0450  20 	b *r0
8365               	;
8366               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
8367               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
8368               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
8369               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
8370               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
8371               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
8372               	; 	'
8373               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
8374               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
8375               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
8376               	; 	'
8377               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
8378               	; 	'   +1  BLACK   Kelly -- and the escalator's FOOT, which the manual
8379               	; 	'               draws black anyway, so the diagonal spans both rows
8380               	; 	'   +2  WHITE   Harry
8381               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line
8382               	; 	'
8383               	; 	' Four is the floor: the Kop, the crook, the furniture and the floor
8384               	; 	' lines are four different colours and none of them can share. Shrinking
8385               	; 	' from six bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the instrument and
8386               	; 	' four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor above it or
8387               	; 	' the bottom of the screen.
8388               	; 	'
8389               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
8390               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
8391               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
8392               	; scan_furn:

8610               	;
8611               	; 	' THE ELEVATOR MARKER TRACKS THE CAR, which is the whole point of it. It
8612               	; 	' used to be a static bar on all three shopping floors -- that says where
8613               	; 	' the shaft is, which never changes and which the player already knows,
8614               	; 	' and says nothing about the one fact that matters: whether the car is
8615               	; 	' where you are. Erase, move, redraw, exactly like an actor's dot.
8616               	; 	' ONE ROW NOW, AND WIDER FOR IT. The band is four pixel rows and each
8617               	; 	' one carries a single colour, so the car's grey row is the same row the
8618               	; 	' escalator heads use; spending a second row on it would put half the
8619               	; 	' car on Kelly's BLACK row, where it would read as the Kop. Five pixels
8620               	; 	' across at a fixed centre column tells it from a three-pixel actor dot
8621               	; 	' without needing the height.

8818               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out

8821               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that

9128               	; 	' used as the shift directly. It was used directly, which MIRRORED every

9774 E608 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     E60A AA0A     
9775               	;
9776               	; 	' REPEATED ADDITION, not a multiply, and deliberately so. Two separate
9777               	; 	' hazards meet on this one line: `tsec * bmul.` is invalid because the `.`
9778               	; 	' suffix marks a 16-bit CONSTANT and both of these are variables, and a
9779               	; 	' plain `tsec * bmul` would be an 8-bit product -- a 50-second capture in
9780               	; 	' the x300 band pays 15,000, which is six times past a byte. Multiplying
9781               	; 	' properly would then land on the MPY/r0 hazard, where a 16-bit var read
9782               	; 	' straight after being multiplied returns the product's HIGH word.
9783               	; 	'
9784               	; 	' At most 50 adds, once per capture. Nothing about this is a hot path.
9785               	; 	'
9786               	; 	' The guard matters: a COMPUTED `FOR 1 TO 0` still runs its body once in
9787               	; 	' CVBasic, so catching Harry on the very last tick would pay a full
9788               	; 	' multiplier instead of nothing.
9789               	; calc_bonus:
9790               cvb_CALC_BONUS
9791               	; 	#bonus = 0

     E60E 20D4

     E626 2146

10025               	;
10026               	; 	' ======================================================================
10027               	; 	' SOUND
10028               	; 	' ======================================================================
10029               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
10030               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
10031               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
10032               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
10033               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
10034               	; sfx_tick:

10382               	;
10383               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
10384               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
10385               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
10386               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
10387               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10388               	; 	'
10389               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
10390               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
10391               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
10392               	; 	'
10393               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
10394               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
10395               	; 	' letter after it.
10396               	;
10397               	; font_bits:
10398               cvb_FONT_BITS
10399               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space

10517               	;
10518               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
10519               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
10520               	; 	'
10521               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
10522               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
10523               	; 	' being small sprites.
10524               	; 	'
10525               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
10526               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
10527               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
10528               	; 	'
10529               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
10530               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
10531               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10532               	;
10533               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly RIGHT then LEFT: hat, face, tunic, leg1, leg2, duck-hat, duck-face. Patterns 0..52
10534               cvb_SPR_KELLY

11184               	;
11185               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
11186               	; 	' Chars 160-207: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
11187               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
11188               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
11189               	;
11190               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
11191               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
11192               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
11193               	; 	' unreadable.
11194               	;
11195               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then furniture grey / Kelly black / Harry white / floor line yellow per 4 px level, on dark green
11196               cvb_SCAN_COL3

11265               	; 	'   156 SCANBK
11266               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
11267               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
11268               	; 	'
11269               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
11270               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
11271               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
11272               	; 	'
11273               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
11274               	; 	' blit is
11275               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
11276               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
11277               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
11278               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
11279               	; 	'
11280               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
11281               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
11282               	; 	'
11283               	; 	' template ids:
11284               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
11285               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
11286               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
11287               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
11288               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
11289               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
11290               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
11291               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
11292               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
11293               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
11294               	;
11295               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
11296               cvb_STOR_TPL
11297               	; 	DATA BYTE 148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148,148,148,98,148,148,148,148
